This motif is essential for substrate recognition of tRNA.<br>- **Zinc-binding residues**: The presence of &quot;HPDE&quot; and cysteine residues (e.g., &quot;CLYCAREL&quot;) is consistent with the zinc-dependent catalytic mechanism of adenosine deaminases.<br>- **Protein length (~250 aa)**: This matches ADAT1 (tRNA-specific adenosine deaminase), which is approximately 236-250 amino acids.<br><br>Evaluating alternatives:<br>- Option B (Cytosine-to-uracil editing in tRNA): Cytidine deaminases have different structural motifs and lack the GRG signature.<br>- Option C (Adenosine-to-inosine editing in mRNA): ADAR proteins are much larger (&gt;1000 aa) with multiple double-stranded RNA-binding domains — not consistent with this ~250 aa protein.<br>- Option D (Methylation of ribosomal RNA): Methyltransferases have completely different catalytic motifs (e.g., Rossmann-fold or SET domain).<br><br>The combination of the GRG motif, appropriate protein size, and zinc-binding architecture strongly indicates this is a tRNA-specific adenosine deaminase (ADAT1), which catalyzes adenosine-to-inosine editing at the wobble position (position 34) of tRNA.<br><br>2. answer: A</td>
